Appendix Additional Services
B

--- PDF page 448 ---

Besides the core AWS tools covered in the book so far, AWS
is constantly adding entirely new services to meet specific and
even niche needs. You may not need to understand the deep- in-
the- weeds technical details of all of those smaller services for your everyday work, but being
aware they exist can make it easy to be confident that your solutions are always right.
What follows in this appendix is brief descriptions of all those services that might show
up on the Solutions Architect Associate exam but that weren’t mentioned anywhere else in
the book.
Deployment Tools
AWS Amplify
In one sense, Amplify is a bit like Elastic Beanstalk. After all, both services exist to help
developers deploy their code without having to bother with all the underlying complexity.
The key difference, though, is that Beanstalk seeks to take your code and make everything
else completely disappear, whereas Amplify lets you invite your full stack team members to
work within the tool using either the Amplify CLI or the browser- based Studio.
Once there, your team will have the tools to quickly build full backend resources—
including databases, storage, and authentication—a nd to design frontend UIs. Staging and
production- hosted deployments happen within the Amplify environment, as does integration
with AWS services like CloudFormation. And entire Amplify project environments can be
exported as templates and redeployed within your own DevOps tooling.
AWS Serverless Application Repository
We all know that repurposing existing resources can be far more efficient than reinventing
the wheel for each new project. So having access to many hundreds of working AWS- based
serverless applications from which to choose can go a long way to speed up your deploy-
ments. AWS Serverless Application Repository already contains more than 1,000 publicly
available apps— from Alexa skills to Lambda integrations with the Stripe payment platform.
You’re free to explore existing apps to learn how to create your own, or to use any of
the code as components in your own deployments. You can also publish your own code and
either make it public or share it privately among your own team members.

--- PDF page 449 ---

Developer Tools 407
AWS Proton
Proton lets organizations create Service Templates that define the AWS compute, storage, net-
working, and CI/CD infrastructure tools that they want for their applications. The organiza-
tion’s developers can then select an existing Service Template, specify a source code origin,
and deploy a new application.
The idea is that organizations can automate applying standards and capacity limits to
their workloads. Rather than forcing the developers themselves to find and launch the AWS
resources they need— and risking them sometimes getting it wrong—i nfrastructure choices
will already be made for them.
Developer Tools
Amazon API Gateway
If you need to build an application that consumes data or other resources existing within the
AWS platform, you can make reliable and safe connections using either RESTful or
WebSocket APIs. API Gateway lets you publish data coming out of just about any AWS ser-
vice for use in your IoT device fleets, web or mobile applications, or monitoring dashboards.
API Gateway is, of course, deeply integrated with all of your existing AWS resources
and will deliver secure and reliable availability and performance at whatever scale you can
throw at it.
AWS Device Farm
A common problem for web and mobile developers is that it can be hard to predict how
their applications will work on each of the many web browsers and mobile devices their
clients may use. Something that works fine on the developer’s own machine may behave
very differently on a web browser that’s a few years out-of-date or on a cutting- edge
Android tablet.
Device Farm is a tool that you can use to test your applications on virtual instances of
more than 100 smartphones and tablets from all major manufacturers. You can similarly test
on multiple versions of mainstream browsers. You test your app performance against virtual
apps using Automated Testing. But you can also test on actual physical devices through
Device Farm’s Remote Access.
AWS Step Functions
Step Functions is a low-c ode platform that lets you design and deploy distributed appli-
cations and process automations through a visual workflow interface. Users with limited

--- PDF page 450 ---

408 Appendix B ■ Additional Services
coding experience can create workflows that integrate multiple AWS services— including
Lambda, ECS, Fargate, DynamoDB, and SNS.
By building applications that incorporate sophisticated error handling, reliability, and
scalability, you could, for instance, create a complete e- commerce platform that controls
everything from inventory control and credit card processing to shipping.
Infrastructure Tools
AWS Outposts
When you require high levels of connectivity with the AWS resources in your region, but
also need an on- premises footprint as part of a hybrid architecture, AWS can deploy physical
servers in your office or datacenter. Outpost deployments can consist of full 42U racks or
individual servers that, once power and network connectivity are provided, will completely
integrate into your infrastructure.
Once connected, you’ll be able to run AWS workloads involving services like EC2, ECS,
EBS, S3, RDS, and more—b ut all with extremely low- latency access rates. In addition, your
cloud-b ased VPCs can be extended to your local site.
As you might imagine, the service isn’t cheap. As of this writing, the monthly no up- front
cost of a 1U server is more than $500, while the all up- front cost starts above $17,000. 42U
racks start at more than $5,000 monthly, or $169,000 all up front.
AWS Wavelength
If individual components of your application require extremely low- latency connectivity
within the 5G mobile network, then you can host those components in Wavelength. This
can be critical for deployments that stream video at high bandwidth, like those involving
connected vehicles and augmented reality.
Wavelength connectivity can be configured through an existing VPC or through a new
virtual network device called a Carrier Gateway, which allows for full connectivity between
the 5G and regular VPC-b ased parts of your application.
VMware Cloud on AWS
VMware’s vSphere is a virtualization platform that lets you build private cloud environments
on your own on- premises equipment. However, private clouds are more and more likely to
incorporate at least some components run on public clouds. Getting such hybrid configu-
rations right can be complex. With that in mind, VMware and AWS collaborated to create
VMware Cloud on AWS.

--- PDF page 451 ---

Connectivity Tools 409
The service provides a specially optimized EC2 instance class (i3en.metal for VMware
Cloud) along with their Virtual Desktop Infrastructure (VDI) to bridge the gap between the
VMware and AWS interface styles. Customers can use an Elastic Network Interface (ENI) to
connect their on- premises vSphere infrastructure to their AWS-b ased resources in a VPC.
Connectivity Tools
Amazon Pinpoint
Pinpoint can automate your inbound and outbound interactions with any number of
marketing contacts. The service takes data you store or generate on S3, Redshift, EC2,
Lambda, or EMR and converts it to notification events using email, SMS, or push notifica-
tions through communications channels. That might mean sending transactional messages in
response to, say, a recent sign- up, or preset information updates to only segments of a cus-
tomer group whose profiles meet certain conditions. Recipient behavior is tracked and ana-
lyzed for engagement, and full reports are generated.
AWS Transfer Family
Existing document and data flows can be easily rerouted for integration with your AWS
resources through either S3 or Amazon EFS. Transfer Family allows you to use the SFTP,
FTPS, or FTP protocols to manage file transfers between AWS and outside data providers or
consumers. The service allows for content management, document sharing, and data distri-
bution operations with third- party partners using industry- standard protocols. It also allows
you to apply the full range of AWS data analytics to data stores that might recently have
existed only outside the cloud.
AWS AppSync
AppSync allows you to build GraphQL APIs to securely and intelligently connect frontend
services with AWS data resources residing in multiple services and formats. You can shape
requests to dynamically filter results and structure the data that’s returned. AppSync can
provide advanced collaboration and real-t ime data streams and can manage connections
that unpredictably go offline, ensuring that data at both ends always ends up properly
synchronized.

--- PDF page 452 ---

410 Appendix B ■ Additional Services
Database Tools
Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB Compatibility)
If you’re looking to achieve the flexibility and simplicity of a MongoDB workload in the
AWS cloud, you’ll turn to DocumentDB. A “document database” is a way or organizing data
in plain-text documents. The MongoDB paradigm uses machine- friendly JSON formatting
for its structure, providing the core for an efficient index- based search experience.
When deployed within Amazon DocumentDB, such a JSON data store is fully managed,
massively scalable, durable, and (as of this writing) compliant with the Apache 2.0 open
source MongoDB 3.6 and 4.0 APIs.
Amazon Keyspaces (for Apache Cassandra)
Cassandra is a NoSQL database management system that distributes large data stores across
clusters of servers. The goal is to achieve high availability with no single point of failure. One
important benefit of Cassandra databases is ultra- low latency access.
Keyspaces is a managed service that lets you import existing Cassandra projects, or create
new ones, within your AWS account that incorporate Cassandra Query Language code and
tools. Keyspaces handles infrastructure, updates, and scaling, while you’re responsible only
for your data.
Amazon Quantum Ledger Database (QLDB)
Maintaining the security and verifiability of large data stores requires being able to track all
changes. Amazon’s QLDB can give you complete cryptographic records of the state of your
data throughout its event history. The goal is to be able to definitively confirm the integrity
of records of, say, insurance claims, asset processing, or credit card transactions.
QLDB is a component of Amazon’s enterprise tool, Blockchain on AWS.
Data Streaming Tools
Amazon Managed Streaming for Apache Kafka (MSK)
Apache Kafka is a distributed messaging system for fault- tolerant and high- throughput mes-
sage exchange. In Kafka, producers send messages to a topic, which is then replicated across
a configurable number of servers. Consumers, when they’re available, read incoming mes-
sages from the topic in sequence.
You use Amazon MSK to efficiently integrate highly scalable Kafka clusters into your
AWS resource stack. This lets you leverage Kafka functionality within the AWS environment

--- PDF page 453 ---

Data Streaming Tools 411
where your operations are already happening. It also gives you full access to the power AWS
data processing and storage tools can add to your Kafka logging and other data operations.
Amazon MQ
Amazon MQ is a message broker service that builds and maintains Apache ActiveMQ and
RabbitMQ messaging brokering flows. The idea is that, in many cases, you can simplify your
application migrations to AWS by importing your code and configurations without having to
retool the way they connect to common messaging protocols and APIs.
MQ provides highly available, durable, and fully managed messaging between applica-
tion layers.
AWS Data Exchange
Data Exchange is a single interface where you can find, subscribe to, and consume third-
party data sets (some of which are available for free). There are currently more than
3,000 data sets available, covering domains like healthcare, technology, financial services,
and manufacturing.
The service connects you to a data source, handles subscriptions, and exposes the data to
any AWS analytics or machine learning tool in your account.
Amazon Timestream
Timestream is built to manage very large data streams consisting of records of (effectively)
limitless events that must each be analyzed. Part of Timestream’s efficiency is realized by
moving data back and forth between memory and cheaper long- term storage based on
demand. Timestream is, as a managed and scalable service, optimized for both performance
and cost. It can be ideal for use cases like managing feedback streams from IoT device
clusters.
AWS Data Pipeline
The regular movement of large data streams or collections between AWS services (or bet-
ween AWS and your on-p remises resources) can be reliably automated using Data Pipeline.
Data— at any scale— can be accessed where it lives and processes, with the output sent to
endpoints in services like S3, DynamoDB, or EMR.
Amazon AppFlow
Data that’s produced using third- party applications like Salesforce, Zendesk, or Slack can be
processed and transferred into AWS services like S3 and Redshift. Scalable data flows using
AppFlow can be configured (without the need for code); transformed through calculation,

--- PDF page 454 ---

412 Appendix B ■ Additional Services
filtering, and masking tools; validated; and ingested into your AWS account resources for
further processing or long- term storage.
Machine Learning and
Artificial Intelligence
Amazon Comprehend
Amazon Comprehend offers natural-l anguage processing (NLP) to analyze text for meaning,
intent, and actionable insights. Large-s cale collections of text from sources like support
tickets, business communications, or social media histories can be kept in Amazon S3
buckets and processed for insights by Comprehend. The resulting data can then be moved to
a data environment like Amazon Redshift for further analytics.
Amazon Forecast
Forecast is a machine-l earning- based service that’s optimized for intelligently identifying
significant attributes within large stores of historical business data. Forecast can automati-
cally select the ideal algorithm for training and building a forecasting model customized for
your data that will surface important trends. Output can be delivered as CSV files, visualized
in the Forecast console, or accessed through the Forecast API.
Amazon Lex
Amazon Lex is a platform for designing and building chatbots using artificial intelligence
and NLP to facilitate live, real-t ime text conversations. Lex chatbots can be deployed on
your website to lower the cost and complexity of customer support operations.
Amazon Polly
Amazon Polly generates speech from text (Text- to- Speech), Neural Text-t o- Speech (which
uses machine learning tools to add styles and tone to speech) and, through Amazon Polly
Brand Voice, custom-d esigned voices that can be used to differentiate your organiza-
tion’s image.
Polly is often used to provide website visitors with alternate ways to consume text- based
content, and for adding speech to applications.

--- PDF page 455 ---

Other Tools 413
Amazon Rekognition
Rekognition’s machine learning analysis API can be incorporated into your image- or video-
processing workflow to derive characteristics and insights from millions of images. It’s cus-
tomizable but provides ready-t o- use, pretrained models for image consumption at any scale.
Rekognition can be used to filter for insecure or inappropriate content, or for sophisticated
environment monitoring solutions.
Amazon Textract
Amazon Textract uses artificial intelligence to extract text from handwritten or printed doc-
uments populated by text, numbers, or even tables. Textract is an optical character recogni-
tion (OCR) tool, but it adds significant sophistication to the OCR technologies in use over
the past 30 years or more. Textract, for instance, tries to understand relationships between
characters and an overall document structure before rendering results.
Textract can be used to improve and speed up workflows involving processing printed or
PDF documents.
Amazon Transcribe
For workflows that involve converting the spoken word to digital text at scale, you’ll use
Amazon Transcribe. You can use Transcribe to analyze call center conversations, summarize
remote meetings, or simplify record keeping for medical professionals.
Amazon Translate
If you need to present your content in multiple languages— and in particular if you can’t
easily predict which languages might be needed—y ou can integrate the Amazon Translate
API to generate real-t ime translations involving (at present) 14 language pairs.
Other Tools
AWS Batch
If you periodically run compute-i ntensive operations— like financial market analyses or
DNA- based drug screening operations—t hen you can configure AWS Batch to automate
all the steps necessary to complete your task whenever triggered by new data inputs. You
schedule Batch to launch all the AWS resources your jobs will need. Those services might
include EC2 (often using Spot Instances) and Fargate. Generally, operations will consume
data from data and storage resources like S3 or DynamoDB.

--- PDF page 456 ---

414 Appendix B ■ Additional Services
AWS X- Ray
Getting regular— and useful— insights into the way your multitier or microservices appli-
cations are running can make the difference between success and failure. But figuring out
exactly where and why failures, bottlenecks, and network latency occur can be tricky.
If your application uses the EC2, ECS, Lambda, SQS, SNS, and Elastic Beanstalk services,
you can use X-R ay to view context- based, real-t ime visibility into everything that’s happen-
ing between your application and its users. X- Ray traces can show you what’s happening at
every step between a user request and termination, whether successful or not. You can use
associated metadata to tag and filter results to help you better visualize relationships and
patterns.
Amazon Kendra
Kendra is a managed AWS service that provides search functionality for users (either
public or internal) of your websites and other hosted content. It’s a machine learning tool,
so it returns far more sophisticated results that simple matching strings. Kendra’s natural
language capacity can derive intelligent analyses and summaries from indexed content
contained in a wide range of document types and locations. Supported platforms include S3,
RDS, Salesforce, and Microsoft SharePoint.
Amazon OpenSearch Service (Amazon
Elasticsearch Service)
Like Kendra, OpenSearch is designed to provide sophisticated search features for large data
sets spread across enterprise sites. Unlike Kendra, OpenSearch does not use natural language
machine learning (in fact, OpenSearch searches are based on SQL queries), and search results
are restricted to string matches. However, it’s much better suited for complex interactive ana-
lytics of data sets like system logs or application monitoring.
OpenSearch, which is not a managed service, requires you to configure and manage an
instance cluster. OpenSearch is derived from the open source Elasticsearch project, version
7.10.2. OpenSearch is specifically optimized for the AWS platform and has added custom
features beyond what’s found in Elasticsearch.
Amazon Managed Grafana
If your monitoring and log visualization operations involve the open source Grafana tool,
you can use Amazon Managed Grafana to incorporate complete functionality into your
AWS account. That means having full access to data sources both inside AWS (like Amazon
CloudWatch and Amazon Elasticsearch Service) and elsewhere (like Datadog and Splunk).
Administrating, querying, visualizing, and analyzing of Amazon Managed Grafana all
take place from within the AWS Console.

--- PDF page 457 ---

Other Tools 415
Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus
Monitoring and managing alerts for large clusters of containers or general infrastructure
can be handled by the Cloud Native Computing Foundation’s Prometheus. The Prometheus
query language (PromQL) can be especially effective for automating workflows.
The Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus service offers a managed and scalable
integration of Prometheus with Amazon’s Elastic Kubernetes Service, Elastic Container Ser-
vice, and Distro for OpenTelemetry services.

Online Test Bank
To help you study for your AWS Certified Solutions Architect Associate certification
exam, register to gain one year of FREE access after activation to the online
interactive test bank— included with your purchase of this book! All of the practice
questions in this book are included in the online test bank so you can study in a
timed and graded setting.
Register and Access the Online Test Bank
To register your book and get access to the online test bank, follow these steps:
1. Go to www.wiley.com/go/sybextestprep. You’ll see the “How to Register Your Book
for Online Access” instructions.
2. Click “here to register” and then select your book from the list.
3. Complete the required registration information, including answering the
security verification to prove book ownership. You will be emailed a pin code.
4. Follow the directions in the email or go to www.wiley.com/go/sybextestprep.
5. Find your book on that page and click the “Register or Login” link with it. Then
enter the pin code you received and click the “Activate PIN” button.
6. On the Create an Account or Login page, enter your username and password, and
click Login or, if you don’t have an account already, create a new account.
7. At this point, you should be in the test bank site with your new test bank listed
at the top of the page. If you do not see it there, please refresh the page or log out
and log back in.
